華泰 (2329 TT)  Trading Buy

獲利逐步增加，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：華泰目前 IC 封測業務佔營收 58%，受惠 NAND Flash 
轉為賣方市場與 AI 儲存需求，封測稼動率回升至約 70%；EMS
業務佔 42%， 主要由佔其營收逾 70% 的美系 AI 伺服器核心客戶
拉貨所帶動。隨全球資料中心算力升級， AI 伺服器出貨量維持
增長， 2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在 AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 
一條龍封測與模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027
全年之營運動能與獲利能見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分
別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。華泰因美系大客戶公司的治理與法規合
規爭議大，再加上記憶體封測的評價較低，因此華泰的整體本
益比低於同業。但華泰隨著 2H26 ASP 提升和新產品貢獻，獲利
逐步增加，因此投資為 Trading Buy ，目標價 50.6 元。  
2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.63 元：記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導體營收回
升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中
心則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳。華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元，
QoQ+12.67% ，其中半導體事業部 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ， 半導體事業產品營收比重分別為 ： 記憶體佔 72%、
邏輯 IC 佔 16% 及測試服務佔 12% 。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD 
controller ，佔全公司營收比重增加至 58% 。 EMS 部份，營收為  
23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。 2Q26 毛
利率來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 。 2Q26 毛利率 13.25%
較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點， 2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.62 元。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元： 華泰過去兩年的年度
資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億
元，加計新建廠房與設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過
25 億元。為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並因應高階封測需求激
增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠房正全面
進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。公司採取
審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓
層建置高等級無塵室，

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

2 群益投顧 
  華泰 ESG ：  
華泰一直以來所秉持的企業核心價值為「誠正信實、主動創新、異體同心、
積極用心」 ，落實企業誠信及企業道德標準是企業永續治理的關鍵，透過有
效的公司治理架構與董事會職能強化公司治理之作為以不斷創造企業的價
值；除外，公司設有薪酬委員會以保障員工福利並維繫公司競爭力，營運過
程以內控制度確保相關程序之正確性也是營運透明化很重要的基礎。  
環境方面，華泰電子在營運上推行許多節省能資源、減少廢棄物以及妥當處
理廢污水、廢棄物的措施。未來也將持續關注氣候變遷與全球暖化議題，對
於溫室氣體排放進行系統化管理與統計，共同維護自然環境、為地球永續盡
一份心力。  
華泰電子極力追尋為員工創造優質職場環境以及專業能力的培訓。不僅是
人才的培育、員工的身心狀態的健康，期望能創造出幸福的健康職場，展現
華泰人的活力。  
華泰主要業務為 IC 封裝與測試製造服務、與專業電子代工製造服務：  
華泰成立於 06/1971 ，為電子產品製造服務和積體電路封裝測試製造服務供
應商，擁有成品事業中心和半導體事業中心兩大事業部門。半導體事業以
NAND Flash 封裝測試為主；成品事業部則為 EMS 代工，以伺服器為主，
SSD 在近年也成為主力之一，全公司目前 5,800 人。頎邦 (6147 TT) 為最大股
東，持股 35.56% 。  
華泰電子製造服務 (EMS/CEM) 服務項目涵蓋：印刷電路板佈線 (PCB 
Layout) 、製造最佳化設計 (DFM) 、測試最佳化設計 (DFT) 、快速原型組裝
(Prototype) 、印刷電路板組裝量產 (PCB Assembly) 、功能性 /可靠度測試
(Function &Reliability Testing) 、系統組裝 (System integration) 等。積體電路
(IC) 封裝測試服務涵蓋：積體電路 (IC) 及各種半導體零組件封裝測試。公司
自 2003 年開始進行產品線及市場定位調整，已將半導體營運重心聚焦於
NAND Flash 各項應用產品之封裝測試。並引進策略性股東金士頓，也是華
泰 NAND Flash 的主要客戶 ； 而EMS 的主要客戶則為 Supermicro(SMCI US) ，
其佔 EMS 約 70% 營收。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元， QoQ+12.67% ，稅後淨利 4.08 億元，
QoQ+73.97% ，稅後 EPS 0.62 元：  
半導體事業中心受惠記憶體產業成為賣方市場，記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導
體營收回升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中心
則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳，再加上記憶體客戶備貨積極，一條龍生產企
業級  SSD 模組，使 2Q26 營運持續向上。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元 ，QoQ+12.67% ， 其中半導體事業部因 NAND Flash
客戶訂單增加明顯， NAND Flash 價格持續上升，華泰的模組客戶積極擴大
下單。半導體事業部營運展現成長趨勢， 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ，半導體事業產品營收比重分別為：記憶體佔 72%、邏輯 IC 佔
16% 及測試服務佔 12%。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD controller ，因此半導體
的封測是以記憶體為主 。 2Q26 半導體事業佔全公司營收比重由 1Q26 的 56% 
增加至 58% 。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

3 群益投顧 
2Q26 EMS 部份，主要營收來源以伺服器、工業電腦與消費性電子為主，其
中伺服器相關產品受惠  AI 伺服器與資料中心算力建設強勁拉貨，美系核
心 AI 伺服器客戶訂單佔整體 EMS 營收比重超過 70% ，承製  GPU/CPU 主
機板與周邊加速板 (SMT/PCBA) 維持高速出貨，帶動 EMS 業績穩健攀升。
2Q26 EMS 營收為  23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。  
2Q26 毛利率增加 ， 來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 ， 產能利用率約 70%
和 1Q26 相近，因為產能在持續擴充中，產能利用率提升較慢。 2Q26 毛利
率 13.25% 較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點，但較 2Q25 減少 1.72 個百分點，最
主要是折舊費用增加，和產品組合改變， single die 封測佔比提升 (消費性電
子降低容量，堆疊的量較低 )， 2Q26 稅後淨利 4.08 億元， QoQ+73.97% ，稅
後 EPS 0.62 元。  
營運資金效率同步提升，負債比率由 1Q26 的 49.3% 大幅降至 38.44% ，應
收帳款週轉天數自 1Q26 的 87 天降至 74 天 ， 存貨週轉天數亦由1Q26 的  59 
天改善至 48 天。  
2Q26 股東權利大幅增加，部份來自子公司持有頎邦約 30,320 張，約佔頎邦
持股 4%，1H26 認列於其他綜合損益之金融資產未實現評價利益 (FVOCI) 約
增加 50.5 億元，惟考量該部份為未實現評價利益，且頎邦股價後續仍可波
動，因此 2026 年底股東權益採較保守假設。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元：  
華泰過去兩年的年度資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 2024 年約 19.59 億元、
2025 年達 23.16 億元。 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億元，加計新建廠房與
設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元。資本支出重點包含高度
自動化晶圓薄化、多晶粒垂直堆疊等高階高容量記憶體封裝設備。建置新廠
之覆晶封裝 (Flip Chip) 與系統級封裝 (SiP) 專用生產線。 2027 年資本支出預
算額度，將待年底董事會審議後定案。  
產能擴充時程與硬體資本支出規劃方面，為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並
因應高階封測需求激增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠
房正全面進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。該新廠總樓
地板面積約達 7 萬平方公尺，公司採取審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階
段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓層建置高等級無塵室，主力配置覆晶封裝 (Flip 
Chip) 及系統級封裝 (SiP) 全自動化產線，並承接自企業總部騰挪出之覆晶產
能，騰出之總部空間則可彈性擴充傳統打線封裝機台 (Wire Bonder) ，以承接
一線封測大廠產能挪移至先進封裝所釋出之成熟邏輯  IC 外溢訂單。  
2Q26 毛利率較 2Q25 同期略為承壓，主因之一即為設備與新廠投資所衍生。
2024 年約 8.34 億元、 2025 年達 9.75 億元。 1H26 約 5.55 億元。 9 層樓新廠
採取分樓層逐步啟用的模式，正是為避免一次性提列過高折舊成本，以維護
資本支出與折舊費用的健康平衡。  
預估華泰 2026 年營收 215.74 億元， YoY+9.61% ，稅後獲利 15.34 億元，
YoY+8.77% ，稅後 EPS 2.33 元：  
先前華泰半導體封測與 EMS 代工業務營收比重約各為 60% ～ 70% 與 30% ～
40%，整體獲利動能與半導體封測景氣循環緊密連動。以往封測代工代價談

### Page 4

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

4 群益投顧 
判為後段產業常態，平均銷售單價 (ASP) 具競爭壓力，惟隨 AI 帶動封測需求
增加，大廠訂單外溢，高階儲存需求激增與原廠比重持續拉升，機台稼動率
提升對毛利率之正向營運槓桿效益顯著；加上公司已於 2H26 針對部分緊俏
封測產能微幅調漲價格，有助獲利結構持續優化。  
華泰半導體事業中心因應邊緣裝置與高階資料傳輸對巨量頻寬與極致縮裝
的需求，全面推進次世代封裝技術架構升級。公司目前主力發展之複合鍵合
/混合封裝， Hybrid Bonding ，整合打線 Wire Bond 與覆晶 Flip Chip 之多晶
粒 SiP 系統級封裝技術 )，已成功量產並出貨應用於  micro SD 7.0 、eUFS 4.x
及 uSSD 等高階記憶體產品，將記憶體晶粒 (Memory Die) 透過打線堆疊、主
控晶片 (Controller) 透過覆晶技術共同整合封裝於單一模組內 ， 作為未來高附
加價值產品線之核心成長動能。在次世代先進封裝中長期研發方面，華泰亦
深化與核心客戶合作，積極開發扇出型覆晶晶圓級封裝 (Fan-Out FCCSP) 等
高階架構，預計於 4Q26 至 2027 年逐步推進至工程試產階段，卡位高速邊
緣運算與客製化 ASIC 儲存市場。  
目前已有 2 家原廠進入華泰前十大客戶名單，並陸續有新原廠接洽驗證。原
廠級訂單具備量體大、能見度長之特性。低容量 eMMC EOL 紅利，受原廠
產能轉移至高階  AI 儲存影響，成熟低容量 eMMC 陸續進入停產 (EOL) 階
段，缺口由台灣及區域晶片商填補，帶動國內記憶體客戶下單積極。  
EMS 事業中心則維持高速成長動能，伺服器主機板 (Server PCBA) 與核心客
戶維持長年深度合作，伺服器相關訂單佔 EMS 營收比重達 70% ，其中美系
AI 伺服器核心大客戶 Supermicro 佔整體伺服器營收比重逾 90% ；由於
Supermicro 訂單模式以帶料生產為主 ， 其營收規模與獲利貢獻顯著高於未帶
料之純加工模組。隨全球資料中心算力升級引領  AI 伺服器出貨量維持增
長，2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在  AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 一條龍封測與
模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027 全年之營運動能與獲利能
見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on final JMTBA orders, which we believe
could be among the catalysts for Airtac's and Hiwin's share
prices.
Per the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline orders grew
2% MoM and 65% YoY in August 2026 (vs. -5% MoM/+50% YoY in July 2026), to
¥198bn.
• Domestic orders  -3% MoM/+62% YoY (vs.-8% MoM/+50% YoY     in July), to
¥52bn
• Overseas orders  +5% MoM/+66% YoY (vs. -4% MoM/+51% YoY   in July), to

¥146bn
• By region:
° China: -2% MoM/+62% YoY, to ¥47bn
° Europe: -12% MoM/+44% YoY, to ¥20bn
° North America: +24% MoM/+72% YoY, to ¥50bn
Our view:
• We think the solid YoY growth for overseas orders continues to point to an
upcycle scenario, despite some seasonal volatility  with MoM growth in some
regions,
• On top of overall segment recovery within automation:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should benefit from its own share gains and
incremental contributions from linear motion components. Valuation

appears undemanding at 17x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average since 2020).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show margin expansion in the coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside as the stock now trades at 29x 2027e P/E
(vs. peak cycle valuation of 35-40x).
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

목표주가(하향): 260,000원

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 130: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

2026-09-17

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

## Document 140: AI回报疑虑暂退，标普纳指反弹，Lumentum领涨光通信股，电信股重挫，油价“过山车”

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI回报疑虑暂退，标普纳指反弹，Lumentum领涨光通信股，电信股重挫，油价“过山车”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

标普收创史上次⾼、全周涨超 1% ，纳指连涨四周、创五个⽉最⻓连涨周。 Lumentum 收涨
超 5% ， T-Mobile 跌超 10% 。美元指数连涨四周、创 21 个⽉最⻓周涨；离岸⼈⺠币盘中再破
6.70 、逼近四年⾼位。伊朗扩⼤严惩违规船范围，原油盘中跌近 2% 后转涨，但特朗普宣布
俄罗斯将供应数百万吨柴油后，油价⼀度转跌。⻩⾦涨超 1% 。
摘要：
围绕⼈⼯智能⽀出的恐慌在 24 ⼩时内完成反转。 OpenAI 营收⼝径之争出现新说法，前⼀天被这
条消息砸下去的芯⽚与云服务商当天拿到逢低买盘。
与此同时，特朗普宣布俄罗斯将向全球市场释放柴油供应，盘中冲⾼的油价随之回落，⻓端美
债收益率继续在 24 年⾼位附近徘徊。
道琼斯⼯业平均指数涨 0.83% 收于 51654.95 点，标普 500 指数涨 0.59% 收于 7811.54 点，纳斯达
克综合指数涨 0.64% 收于 27366.17 点，标普 500 与纳斯达克终结两连跌。标普 500 ⼗⼀⼤板块九
涨⼆跌，房地产板块涨 1.88% 领涨，通信服务板块跌 0.40% 垫底。

OpenAI 营收⼝径之争在 24 ⼩时内翻转，周四被砸的芯⽚与云服务商拿到逢低买盘；油价在伊朗
⾰命卫队袭击霍尔⽊兹海峡⼀艘液化⽯油⽓油轮后被推⾼，⼜被特朗普与普京达成的柴油供应
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

协议压了回去，收益率对估值的挤压随之减轻。
⼈⼯智能这⼀侧的变化最明显。前⼀天英国⾦融时报关于 OpenAI 年化营收远低于此前暗⽰⽔平
的报道，把甲⻣⽂、博通、英伟达、 CoreWeave 等⼀整条链砸了下去；新的⼝径说明出来后，
恐慌让位于逢低吸纳，反弹沿着盈利能⻅度最⾼的公司扩散。
地缘这⼀侧的拉锯决定⻛险偏好的上限。伊朗⾰命卫队把打击范围从霍尔⽊兹海峡向海峡外扩
展，也⻔胡塞武装据报在曼德海峡布设⽔雷；特朗普给这场冲突贴了⼀个到期⽇，油价最终只
录得微涨，美股得以把注意⼒放回盈利本⾝。
OpenAI 营收⼝径之争翻转，科技股拿到逢低买盘
周四把⼈⼯智能概念股打下去的是⼀条营收数字，周五把它们托起来的还是这条数字。
华尔街⻅闻此前提及， OpenAI 截⾄ 9 ⽉底的年化营收接近 500 亿美元，⽐市场流传的 680 亿⾄
700 亿美元低了近 200 亿。
随后 OpenAI ⽅⾯澄清，较⾼数字系将通过合作伙伴渠道确认的收⼊合并计算，同⼝径下收⼊已
从约 300 亿美元增⻓⾄ 500 亿美元。据彭博报道， OpenAI 预计年内收⼊将达到或超过 700 亿美

元，这⼀表态令纳斯达克指数收复失地，周内基本收平。
周五微软涨 2.40% ，亚⻢逊涨 3.30% ， Palantir 涨 5.20% ， Datadog 涨 7.08% ，罗素 2000 指数涨
0.50% 收于 2806.98 点。
前⼀天跌得最狠的芯⽚股没有全部收复失地，英伟达仍跌 0.50% 。资⾦挑的是盈利能⻅度，逢低
就买的并不多。
富国银⾏投资研究所全球股票策略师 Doug Beath 提醒，标普 500 的参与⾯⼀直偏窄，这本⾝就
说明投资者认为⼤型科技股更能扛住油价和收益率，对其余板块保持谨慎。他判断，科技板块
⼀旦让⼈失望，触发的不只是惯常的震荡。
⻛险偏好同步回暖的还有欧洲。欧洲斯托克 600 指数涨 1.03% ，英国富时 100 指数涨 1.06% 收于
10552.05 点，德国 DAX 指数涨 1.13% 收于 25087.27 点，法国 CAC40 指数涨 0.95% 收于 7803.33
点。

Source: Bloomberg
Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly
US$6bn. Amid the surging demand, Bloom is improving backlog conversion and
stepping up capacity expansion to capture the strong growth opportunity. In 2Q26,
Bloom raised its full-year revenue guidance to US$3.9-4.2bn, up from US$3.4-3.8bn,
and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 29 Figure 30
ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

GIS-KY(6456 TT)       法說 /座談摘要  
光通訊及 AR/VR 相關新事業有 望 2027 、2028 年帶來顯 著貢獻  
⚫ 營收將自 2Q26 起逐季轉佳  
⚫ 雷射封測試 量產進行中，預期年底 出貨， 2027 將有顯著 貢獻  
結論： GIS-KY 1H26 因終端零組件缺貨導致營收顯著下滑並陷入虧損，
但整體財務結構與淨現金部位依然穩健。展望 2H26，隨著記憶體短缺問
題逐步舒緩，營運預期將逐季回溫。公司目前正積極推動業務轉型，從
傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學領域，並已擴大資本支出佈局光通訊雷射
光源封測與 AR/VR 等新產品 ， 預計這些新事業將於2027 至 2028 年成為
公司顯著的營收成長動能 。

2Q26 財務數據  
⚫ 營收 105 億元(QoQ-34.7% ，YoY-44.8%)  
⚫ 毛利率 3.3%(QoQ -1.7ppts ，YoY-3.6ppts)  
⚫ 營業利益率 -7.6%(QoQ -5.6ppts ，YoY-8.7ppts)  
⚫ 稅後淨利 -10.53 億元(QoQ-415% ，YoY-2592%)  
⚫ 單季 EPS -3.15 元 
產品營收占比  
⚫ 2Q26：平板佔 42%、筆電佔 33%、指紋辨識模組佔 14%、其他佔
11%。 
⚫ 1H26：平板佔 44%、筆電佔 32%、指紋辨識模組佔 15%、其他佔
9%。 
法說會內容摘要  
1. 2Q26 營收下滑主因為公司目前主要出貨產品以平板與筆電為主，
終端客戶受到記憶體等零組件缺貨影響而無法順利拉貨，導致營收
顯著下降。而毛利下降是因為營收縮小，導致折舊等製造與固定費
用無法獲得足夠的營收進行攤銷。  
2. 因 2Q26 進行大陸子公司盈餘匯回母公司的資金安排，須繳納相關
所得稅費用，導致最終稅後淨損達 10.53 億元，淨利率為負 10%。 
3. 公司轉型大方向維持不變，致力於從傳統的觸控顯示模組使用者介
面 (User Interface) ，轉向半導體光學相關元件發展。  
4. 公司將持續佈局 Micro LED、Waveguide、AR HUD 以及 Optical Lens
等產品線，期盼未來能有具體成果展現。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

2

Q&A 內容摘要  
1.Q：3Q26 及 4Q26 的營運展望為何？  
A：7、8 月的單月營收約在 40 億元上下，較 2Q26 單月約 30 億元表現好，主要是記憶體缺貨
情況有稍微緩解。整體來看，3Q26 營收狀況會比 2Q26 好；而記憶體短缺的問題預計在 4Q26
會陸續得到舒緩，因此預估4Q26 營收會相對於 3Q26 更好，具體成長幅度待下次法說會詳細說
明。

2.Q：Sharp 龜山面板廠關廠對公司的影響為何？  
A：夏普是公司平板與筆電 TFT 面板的重要來源，其龜山廠在 8 月底已關廠，確實對 TFT 面板
來源有顯著影響。但終端市場正陸續轉向 OLED 面板，公司前幾年已預判此趨勢並推動轉型，
關廠只是讓此事提前發生 。 面板來源主要影響液晶顯示模組 (LCM) 後段業務 ， 但在Touch Panel
貼合業務上，即使轉用 OLED 仍可應用，受影響不大，同時公司也不排除尋找其他面板來源。

3.Q：最近 9 月初公告的資本支出用途為何？GIS 目前在光通訊領域的投資及產品是什麼？  
A：9 月初董事會核准台灣及越南子公司合計約 33 億元的資本支出，主要用於光通訊雷射光源
的封裝與測試設備，以因應終端龐大的需求。相關設備移入與實際進入量產的時間點，預估會
落在 1H27 之後。

4.Q：2026 年全年的資本支出大概為多少？項目為何？  
A：年初原本預估全年資本支出為 50 至 60 億元，但因客戶需求擴大，目前公告核准的資本支
出總額已達 80 多億元。項目包含光通訊雷射光源封測（約40 至 50 億元），以及新產品量產、
光學、AR 相關投資（約 10 至 20 億元）。這 80 億元不會集中在今年一次性現金流出，而是會
分散在今年與明年陸續發生。

5.Q：下半年是否同上半年一樣會有盈餘匯回母公司產生的稅務費用發生？  
A：公司 6 月初已公告大陸子公司有約 10 億人民幣盈餘要匯回。此金額有一大部分已於上半年
匯回，但另一部分會在下半年匯回，因此下半年的確還是會產生相關所得稅費用，不過金額不
會像上半年那麼龐大與顯著。

6.Q：越南廠目前的投資進度大概如何？ 
 A：越南廠土建即將完工，接下來將進行內部機電設備施工，預計今年底全部完工，年底到明
年初會有部分產品量產。越南廠主要生產大客戶的觸控模組，另有一部分光通訊雷射光源封裝
測試也會在該廠生產；因應物流運籌與策略考量，目前也正與其他客戶協調廠地規劃與產能。

### Page 3

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

3 
7.Q：公司發展的新產品（包含光波導、光通訊等）目前的發展狀況為何？ 
 A：光通訊雷射光源封測的第一階段機台裝機與試量產正在進行，順利的話預計今年底前開始
出貨，明年起會有較顯著營收貢獻。光波導與AR/VR 相關產品正與客戶密切開發中，考量客戶
計畫變化與產品組合要求，預計要到 2027 年甚至 2028 年才會有較顯著的貢獻。

8.Q：關於指紋辨識產品的展望為何？ 
 A：主要提供應用於手機與平板的超聲波與電容式指紋辨識模組。中短期仍受終端零組件（記
憶體）缺貨影響出貨動能，後續若要有顯著營收成長，須期待終端缺貨狀況獲得進一步緩解。

9.Q：請問對明年展望怎麼看？新事業明年的營收貢獻預估會到哪裡？  
A：期望光通訊及 AR/VR 相關新事業在 2027、2028 年能帶來顯著貢獻。但明年具體貢獻程度
因終端市場與客戶動態變化大，可能要等到下一季季底才會比較明朗；但公司整體轉型的發展
方向不會有太大改變。

10.Q：今年在雷射光源預計的資本支出大約是 40 到 50 億元，9 月份公告的是 33 億元，是否代
表先前已有 10 到 20 億元已經投入並且即將投產？  
A：是的，去年底或今年初已陸續投入約 10 多億元於雷射光源封裝，設備已進駐並進行小量測
試與量產爬坡階段，預計 4Q26 就能產生相對的貢獻。9 月公告的 33 億元是後續再增加的擴產
資本支出。

11.Q：雷射光源封裝產能擴充後，預估 2027 年的月產能大概可以達到多少 KK？  
A：因終端市場需求非常強勁，目前公司重點在調校製程良率與生產效率，故不方便提供明確
的月產能數字，待年底將良率與效率調整到位後，再向外界做較完整的報告。

12.Q：雷射光源目前總共有幾個客戶或潛在客戶？在光通訊領域除了雷射光源外是否還有其他
機會？  
A：目前接洽對象以國際大廠為主，有些訂單已確定，有些在洽談中，不便透露具體數量或客
戶名單。期望藉由此次業務在光通訊領域站穩腳步，內部有研發多種新產品，待取得客戶信任
與認證後，會往產業鏈上下游其他相關業務發展。

13.Q：如何看待雷射光源封裝的毛利率？ 
 A：接單前內部都會評估過毛利率，達標才會接單並通過投資案。理論值雖然可以掌握，但實
際毛利率需待進入一定規模量產，產生實際營收與成本費用後才會明確；公司策略上也會優先
承接毛利率較高的高附加價值訂單。

### Page 4

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

202 6 /09 /29

4 
14.Q：雷射光源封裝的 Know-how 與既有業務重疊性高嗎？主要進入門檻為設備還是人員經
驗？  
A：公司過去在電容式指紋辨識等領域已累積了長期的IC 測試與封裝經驗，並獲國際大廠認證
出貨。因此無論是人員、設備、經驗或技術 Know-how 皆具備一定實力，跨入該領域並非單一
因素門檻，而是多種經驗綜合累積的結果。

15.Q：雷射封裝測試上游原料是自行取得還是客戶指定？下游客戶是光模組廠還是CSP 廠？有
無針對特定傳輸規模（如 400G 或 800G）著墨？  
A：上游原料視不同客戶情況而定；下游客戶目前尚未直接接觸到CSP 廠，客戶商業樣態各有
不同。未來目標會以 800G 以上、較高功率與高階的晶片封裝服務來進行佈局。

16.Q：光通訊後續還有機會增加資本投入嗎？以目前 40 到 50 億的資本支出來說，兩三年回本
的機率高嗎？  
A： 期望藉由此次投資在光通訊產業鏈建立客戶信任 ，若未來評估有可行的新領域會持續跨足，
屆時就可能有增加資本投入的需求。公司當然希望能高機率回本，因此重點是提升生產良率與
效率，並穩固客戶關係，達成後回本的機率就會相對較高。

17.Q：雷射技術是以 EML 還是 CW 為主？  
A：這取決於客戶的需求，目前不管 EML 或 CW，公司的技術與能力都可以做到。

Update
Makalot Industrial  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Sales In-Line; Attractive
Entry Point; Keep OW
September sales reached NT$2.93bn (+24% YoY/−18% MoM), driven by shipment
growth of 22% YoY, ASP down 1%, and the balance from FX. Management noted
that shipments worth NT$80mn were delayed to next month due to volatile
shipping schedules.
3Q26 sales (+7% YoY) were broadly in line with both our estimates and consensus.
Shipments rose 1% (slightly below expectations due to shipment delays and order

cherry-picking), ASP was flat, and FX contributed +6%. Based on our simulations,
July and August GPM should exceed 3Q26 guidance of 24%.
Makalot kept 4Q26 guidance unchanged, with shipments expected to grow at a low-
single-digit YoY rate, ASP up 5–7% (supported by cherry-picking higher-ASP, higher-
profit orders), and GPM at 26% (-0.7ppt YoY).
We continue to expect Makalot's 2026 earnings to return close to peak levels. Our
net income forecast of NT$3.9bn is back to the 2023 level and only a low-single-

digit percentage below the record achieved in 2024. We believe macro uncertainties
are already priced in and view the current level as an attractive entry point, with
valuation at 11.3x our 2H26–1H27 EPS estimate and a 7.9% cash yield based on our
projected 2026 dividend.

Update
2
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Makalot Industrial (1477.TW)
Base case value, derived by applying a multiple of 17x to our 2026 EPS estimate. Our target
multiple is below its average P/E ratio in the prior up-cycle of 21x (after re-rating in 2H23), and
is within the stock's 10-year historical 9-29x P/E range. We view this as reasonable, with 2026e
earnings growth of 8% seen outperforming apparel and footwear peers with flat to single-
digit growth.

Risks to Upside
n New non-US customer wins
n Faster-than-expected inventory digestion
n ASP and margin expansion via more complex garments
n Better US GDP growth
n Faster order reallocation owing to trade tension
Risks to Downside
n Deterioration in the macro environment
n Drastic appreciation of TWD
n Pricing competition initiated from competitors
n Tariff increases on apparel exports from Vietnam or Cambodia

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

## Document 253: OpenAI孵化的AI制药独角兽，9个月征服6家跨国药企，捅破行业天花板

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI孵化的AI制药独角兽，9个月征服6家跨国药企，捅破行业天花板.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 制药初创公司 Chai Discovery 成⽴仅两年多，九个⽉内已与礼来、辉瑞、诺华、 BMS 、
GSK 等六家药企达成合作，最新估值达 38 亿美元。与传统 AI 制药公司⾃建管线不同， Chai
专注模型授权和定制开发，凭借实验验证的设计能⼒获得药企认可。 Chai 最早是在
OpenAI 的办公室⾥孵化出来的， OpenAI ⾄今也是它的投资⼈之⼀。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 9 ⽇， AI 制药初创公司 Chai Discovery 官宣与 GSK （葛兰素史克）达成合作。
这看似是⼀笔常规跨国药企的 AI 合作，但值得关注的是，这已经是 Chai 九个⽉内签下的第六家
制药合作伙伴。
礼来、辉瑞、诺华、 argenx 、 BMS ，再加上 GSK ， Chai 已经拿下全球近半数 TOP10 药企。
⽽与它们合作的 Chai ，是⼀家 2024 年才成⽴，到今天还不满三岁的公司。
今年 7 ⽉， Chai Discovery 宣布完成 4 亿美元 C 轮融资，估值达到 38 亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 257 亿

元）。
更有意思的是，Chai ⼿⾥没有⼀条⾃研管线，也没有⼀个分⼦进⼊临床，它对外售出的只有⼀
样东西：模型。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻓期以来，向药企销售软件⼀直被视为天花板极低的业务，营收增⻓有限，但 Chai 似乎打破了
这个看法。
⼀家只卖模型的公司，凭什么让⼤药企排着队签约？
九个⽉，六张 MNC ⻔票
今年年初， Chai 联合创始⼈兼 CEO Josh Meier 曾公开表⽰， 2026 年将是 Chai 的 “ 部署之年 ” 。
梳理合作节奏，可以看出合作曲线很陡峭。从礼来到辉瑞， Chai ⽤了将近五个⽉；⽽从辉瑞到
GSK ，短短四个⽉⾥⼜连签了五家。
不仅如此，礼来在正式签约之前，还先拿 Chai 的⼀批模型设计做了内部评估。
⽽ GSK 也⾮常谨慎，从将信将疑到正式签约，整整花了⼀年。
2025 年 9 ⽉， GSK 研发技术⾼级副总裁、前美国国⽴卫⽣研究院（ NIH ）资深研究负责⼈
Christopher Austin 看完 Chai-2 的论⽂，就和 Chai 管理层⻅了⼀⾯。
他表⽰，那篇论⽂⾮常惊艳，但 “ 我们当然不相信 ” 。
双⽅此后⼀直保持联系，今年 1 ⽉在摩根⼤通医疗健康⼤会上再次碰⾯，敲定由 GSK 对 Chai 的技

术做⼀次独⽴测试， 2 ⽉正式启动。
靶点是 GSK ⾃⼰挑的，都是它感兴趣、也认为 Chai 模型有能⼒处理的项⽬； Chai 只负责交出零
样本⽣成的蛋⽩质序列，合成和实验验证全部在 GSK ⾃⼰的实验室⾥完成。
结果让 GSK 团队印象深刻，其内部湿实验在测试的所有靶点上都找到了能够结合的分⼦设计，
并且都是零样本⽣成的。尤其是有效抗体出现的频率和结合亲和⼒的强度。
Austin 表⽰： “ 我已经学会对这类事情保持怀疑，但这次的结果超出了我的预期。 ”
这位研发负责⼈也直⾔，他们考察了所有做 AI 蛋⽩设计的公司，并且⾮常挑剔。这意味着，⼤药
企愿意掏钱的，从来只是当下最强的那⼀两家。

### Page 3

最终双⽅敲定了正式合作。值得注意的是， Chai 和药企的合作的形式也在悄悄升级。
礼来是 “ 共建专属模型 ” ，辉瑞是 “ 授权使⽤新模型 ” ，到了 BMS ，已经变成把 Chai 的模型整合进抗
体发现管线。
早在今年 6 ⽉， Chai 就透露正在和超过 15 家药企谈判，并计划年内签下更多合作。
GSK ，⼤概率不会是最后⼀家。
只做软件不做管线
Chai Discovery 成⽴于 2024 年 3 ⽉，总部位于美国加利福尼亚州旧⾦⼭，是⼀家致⼒于使⽤ AI 赋
能药物开发的公司。
四位联合创始⼈ Joshua Meier 、 Jack Dent 、 Matthew McPartlon 和 Jacques Boitreaud ，来⾃
OpenAI 、 Meta FAIR 、 Stripe 和 Absci ，是典型的 “AI 原⽣ ” 组合。
公司最早甚⾄是在 OpenAI 的办公室⾥孵化出来的， OpenAI ⾄今也是它的投资⼈之⼀。
成⽴后不久，他们推出了第⼀代结构预测模型 Chai-1 ，在业内打响了名号。但真正让 Chai 从⼀

众 AI 制药公司⾥跳出来的，是 2025 年 6 ⽉发布的 Chai-2 。
按照 Chai 公布的数据， Chai-2 在 52 个全新靶点上做抗体从头设计，每个靶点只测试不超过 20 个
设计，就为⼤约⼀半的靶点找到了结合物，整体命中率在 16% 左右。
今年，它⼜推出了第三代模型 Chai-3 ，据称在⽣成符合治疗标准的抗体上，成功率是上⼀代的
两倍。辉瑞最终拍板签约，看中的正是 Chai-3 的设计能⼒。
过去⼗年， AI 制药公司⾛的⼤多是同⼀条路：⽤ AI 发现分⼦，⾃⼰推进管线，最后靠临床数据和
授权交易兑现价值。
Chai 选了⼀条截然相反的路，核⼼模式均为前沿 AI 平台授权  + 基于药企专有数据的定制模型开
发。
简单⽽⾔ Chai Discovery 不做管线，⽽是将⾃⼰定位为⼀个平台提供商，将 AI 软件平台授权给诺
华、辉瑞、礼来等⼤型药企使⽤。
这种平台型的商业模式，正在吸引⼀众资本的加⼊。
更让⼈意外的是 Chai 的体量。
这家公司⽬前只有将近40 名员⼯，⼏乎全是研究⼈员和科学家，甚⾄没有销售团队，只靠⼀个

⼩型 BD 团队对接药企。
在 Chai 看来，这恰恰说明了问题。
药企签约不是因为 AI 炒作，⽽是因为看到了真实的结果和价值，这样的交易不是每天去拜访药企
研发团队就能谈下来的，它需要能真正推动变⾰的技术。
不仅如此， Chai 还在挑战⼀个⾏业⾥的⽼共识。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
71 收藏 分享到 :
⻓期以来，向药企卖软件被认为是⼀⻔做得成、但天花板很低的⽣意。药企愿意为软件掏的钱
有限，真正的⼤钱，来⾃在药物项⽬⾥占有⼀部分经济权益。
但公司联合创始⼈ Meier 认为，这⼀次不⼀样了， AI 在药物发现中创造的价值已经远超以往，这
会颠覆很多关于商业模式的固有认知。
⽽且需求远没有⻅顶。早在今年 6 ⽉， Chai 就透露正在和超过 15 家药企谈判，并计划年内签下更
多合作。
同样卖模型，为什么国内药企很难买账？
⽽这条路，恰恰是国内 AI 制药最难复制的⼀条。
过去⼏年中国创新药最赚钱的⽅式，是做出⼀个分⼦，再通过 license-out 或 NewCo 卖给海外药
企，所以它们更愿意为管线付钱，⽽不是为软件付费。
再加上国内整体的软件付费习惯本就薄弱，按年订阅⼀个 AI 平台，在⼤多数药企的采购逻辑⾥很

难找到位置。
Chai 最锋利的能⼒是抗体从头设计，价值最⼤的场景是难成药靶点和 first-in-class 项⽬。
⽽国内创新药的主流打法仍以 fast-follow 和 me-better 为主，靶点⼤多已被验证，传统的筛选和优
化流程早已⾜够成熟， AI 从头设计带来的边际价值⾃然有限。
研发预算的量级差距也摆在那⾥，头部 MNC ⼀年的研发投⼊以百亿美元计，国内头部药企是⼏
⼗亿⼈⺠币量级，能拿出来试新⼯具的空间要⼩得多。
再加上融资环境收紧，平台型公司往往被逼着转向⾃建管线或项⽬制服务。⾄于把客⼾做到海
外 MNC ，⼜会撞上⽇益收紧的地缘政治与数据合规审查。
并且随着跨国药企对 AI 企业越来越重视，对合作⽅也会越来越挑剔。国内药企需要有过硬的突破
性成果，以及经过药企湿实验验证的硬证据。
当然，并不是没有国内公司尝试过这条路。晶泰科技、百图⽣科等公司都曾与海外⼤药企签下
平台或研发合作。
但⼀家中国 AI 公司想拿到 MNC 核⼼管线的数据、建⽴ Chai 那样的深度绑定，难度要⼤得多。

所以，真正的变量既在 AI 公司⾝上，⽽在国内药企⾝上。
当国内头部药企开始⼤规模做突破式创新，开始为更难的靶点发愁时， AI 从头设计才会从锦上添
花变成刚需。
本⽂来源：智药局

Portfolio Strategist

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  8

Owing to their ethical framework and asset -backed nature, Sukuk are often
considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

## Document 291: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.
